Overview
EOL14595
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Meeting Server Perpetual Offers. The last day to order the affected product(s) is November 23, 2022. Customers with active service contracts will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract.
This bulletin is only for Cisco Meeting Server perpetual licensing. Existing customers are encouraged to migrate from perpetual licensing to Cisco Meeting Server (A-CMS) Subscription Offer. Cisco is committed to long-term support for our customers with on-premises deployments and continuing product feature updates for Cisco Meeting Server. 
We are also committed to supporting our customers as they move to subscription. Some of the customer benefits of a subscription offer include: 
●Flexible payment methods – monthly, annual, and full
●Software Support that is included - never find yourself uncovered
●Simplified licensing structure
For customers migrating to a subscription offer, a trade-in credit is available for customers with existing Software Support Services (SWSS) contracts for most customers. Contact your sales team for details. After the End of Service renewal date referenced below existing customers will need to migrate to subscription to receive support and software updates. 
For more information about Cisco Meeting Server Subscription Offer, refer to this link: <https://www.cisco.com/go/meetingserver>.
End-of-life milestones
**Table 1.** End-of-life milestones and dates for the Cisco Meeting Server Perpetual Offers  
|  Milestone  |  Definition  |  Date  |  
| --- | --- | --- |  
|  **End-of-Life Announcement Date**  |  The date the document that announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life of a product is distributed to the general public.  |  May 25, 2022  |  
|  **End-of-Sale Date:  
App SW**  |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  November 23, 2022  |  
|  **Last Ship Date:  
App SW**  |  The last-possible ship date that can be requested of Cisco and/or its contract manufacturers. Actual ship date is dependent on lead time.  |  February 21, 2023  |  
|  **End of SW Maintenance Releases Date:  
App SW**  |  The last date that Cisco Engineering may release any final software maintenance releases or bug fixes. After this date, Cisco Engineering will no longer develop, repair, maintain, or test the product software.  |  November 23, 2023  |  
|  **End of New Service Attachment Date:  
App SW**  |  For equipment and software that is not covered by a service-and-support contract, this is the last date to order a new service-and-support contract or add the equipment and/or software to an existing service-and-support contract.  |  November 23, 2023  |  
|  **End of Service Contract Renewal Date:  
App SW**  |  The last date to extend or renew a service contract for the product.  |  February 18, 2025  |  
|  **Last Date of Support:  
App SW**  |  The last date to receive applicable service and support for the product as entitled by active service contracts or by warranty terms and conditions. After this date, all support services for the product are unavailable, and the product becomes obsolete.  |  November 30, 2025  |  
HW = Hardware OS SW = Operating System Software App. SW = Application Software

Product migration options
Legacy Acano Licensing Unit (ACU) to SMP migration is End-of-Sale with this bulletin. Any existing ACU customers requiring SMP should purchase A-CMS subscription and work with the Cisco sales account team for migration pricing options. 
Service prices for Cisco products are subject to change after the product End-of-Sale date.

Overview
EOL13845
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Perpetual Licensing for Cisco Enterprise Agreement for Collaboration Multiparty Telepresence. The last day to order the affected product(s) is August 19, 2021. Customers with active service contracts will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract.
Customers are encouraged to migrate to Cloud Webex meetings under A-FLEX-3. The Cisco Collaboration Flex Plan 3.0 Ordering Guide is available at: <https://www.cisco.com/c/en/us/products/collateral/unified-communications/spark-flex-plan/guide-c07-744224.html>.
Customers who require On Premises Meetings should consider the option of either the Cisco Collaboration Flex Plan subscription (A-FLEX) , or a Cisco Meeting Server Subscription (A-CMS.) The A-FLEX selection provides a choice of either Cloud or On Premises Meetings, including the ability to mix deployments. Alternatively, A-CMS is a pure On Premises solution.
The ordering guides are available below: Cisco Collaboration Flex Plan Ordering Guide: <https://www.cisco.com/c/dam/en/us/products/collateral/customer-collaboration/cisco-collaboration-flex-plan-ordering-guide.pdf>.
Cisco Meeting Server Subscription Ordering Guide: <https://www.cisco.com/c/dam/en/us/products/se/2019/4/WW_Partner_Organization/C07-730707-06_Cisco_Meeting_Server_OG_v2a.pdf>.

App SW **  |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  August 19, 2021   |

App SW **  |  For equipment and software that is not covered by a service-and-support contract, this is the last date to order a new service-and-support contract or add the equipment and/or software to an existing service-and-support contract.  |  August 19, 2022   |

App SW **  |  The last date to extend or renew a service contract for the product.  |  November 15, 2023   |

## Consent Manager
  * ### Your Privacy
  * ### Strictly Necessary Cookies
  * ### Performance Cookies
  * ### Targeting Cookies

#### Your Privacy
When you visit any website, it may store or retrieve information on your browser, mostly in the form of cookies. This information might be about you, your preferences or your device and is mostly used to make the site work as you expect it to. The information does not usually directly identify you, but it can give you a more personalized web experience. Because we respect your right to privacy, you can choose not to allow some types of cookies. From the list on left, please choose whether this site may use Performance and/or Targeting Cookies. By selecting Strictly Necessary Cookies only, you are requesting Cisco not to sell or share your personal data. Note, blocking some types of cookies may impact your experience on the site and the services we are able to offer.
#### Strictly Necessary Cookies
Always Active
These cookies are necessary for the website to function and cannot be switched off in our systems. They are usually only set in response to actions made by you which amount to a request for services, such as setting your privacy preferences, logging in or filling in forms. You can set your browser to block or alert you about these cookies, but some parts of the site will not then work. These cookies do not store any personally identifiable information.
Cookies Details
#### Performance Cookies
Performance Cookies
These cookies provide metrics related to the performance and usability of our site. They are primarily focused on gathering information about how you interact with our site, including: page load times, response times, error messages, and allowing a replay of a visitor’s interactions with our site, which enables us to review and analyze visitor behavior, helping to improve site usability and functionality. These cookies also allow us to count visits and traffic sources so we can measure and improve the performance of our site. They help us to know which pages are the most and least popular and see how visitors move around the site. If you do not allow these cookies we will not know when you have visited our site and will not be able to monitor its performance.
Cookies Details
#### Targeting Cookies
Targeting Cookies
These cookies may be set through our site by our advertising partners. They may be used by those companies to build a profile of your interests and show you relevant adverts on other sites. They do not store directly personal information, but are based on uniquely identifying your browser and internet device. If you do not allow these cookies, you will experience less targeted advertising.
Cookies Details
#### Functional Cookies
Functional Cookies
These cookies enable the website to provide enhanced functionality and personalisation. They may be set by us or by third party providers whose services we have added to our pages. If you do not allow these cookies then some or all of these services may not function properly.
Cookies Details

Overview
EOL13771
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Meeting Server 2000 M5. The last day to order the affected product(s) is August 30, 2021 . Customers with active service contracts will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract.
The CMS 2000 M5 was based upon Intel chipsets that have been superseded. Therefore, the CMS 2000 M5 is being superseded by the CMS 2000 M5 v2 (CTI-CMS2KM52BUN-K9).

HW **  |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  August 30, 2021   |  
|  **Last Ship Date:  
HW **  |  The last-possible ship date that can be requested of Cisco and/or its contract manufacturers. Actual ship date is dependent on lead time.  |  November 28, 2021   |  
|  **End of Vulnerability/Security Support:  
HW **  |  The last date that Cisco Engineering may release a planned maintenance release or scheduled software remedy for a security vulnerability issue.   |  August 29, 2024   |  
|  **End of Routine Failure Analysis Date:  
HW **  |  The last-possible date a routine failure analysis may be performed to determine the cause of hardware product failure or defect.  |  August 30, 2022   |  
|  **End of New Service Attachment Date:  
HW **  |  For equipment and software that is not covered by a service-and-support contract, this is the last date to order a new service-and-support contract or add the equipment and/or software to an existing service-and-support contract.  |  August 30, 2022   |  
|  **End of Service Contract Renewal Date:  
HW **  |  The last date to extend or renew a service contract for the product.  |  November 25, 2025   |  
|  **Last Date of Support:  
HW **  |  The last date to receive applicable service and support for the product as entitled by active service contracts or by warranty terms and conditions. After this date, all support services for the product are unavailable, and the product becomes obsolete.   |  August 31, 2026   |  
HW = Hardware OS SW = Operating System Software App. SW = Application Software

Product migration options
Customers may be able to use the Cisco Technology Migration Program (TMP) where applicable to trade-in eligible products and receive credit toward the purchase of new Cisco equipment. For more information about Cisco TMP, customers should work with their Cisco Partner or Cisco account team. Cisco Partners can find additional TMP information on Partner Central at: <https://www.cisco.com/web/partners/incentives_and_promotions/tmp.html>.
Customers may be able to continue to purchase the Cisco Meeting Server 2000 M5 through the Cisco Certified Refurbished Equipment program. Refurbished units may be available in limited supply for sale in certain countries on a first-come, first-served basis until the Last Date of Support has been reached. For information about the Cisco Certified Refurbished Equipment program, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/go/eos>.
Service prices for Cisco products are subject to change after the product End-of-Sale date.
The Cisco Takeback and Recycle program helps businesses properly dispose of surplus products that have reached their end of useful life. The program is open to all business users of Cisco equipment and its associated brands and subsidiaries. For more information, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/web/about/ac227/ac228/ac231/about_cisco_takeback_recycling.html>.
For more information
For more information about the Cisco End-of-Life Policy, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/en/US/products/products_end-of-life_policy.html>.
For more information about the Cisco Product Warranties, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/en/US/products/prod_warranties_listing.html>.
To subscribe to receive end-of-life/end-of-sale information, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/cisco/support/notifications.html>.

## Consent Manager
  * ### Your Privacy
  * ### Strictly Necessary Cookies
  * ### Performance Cookies
  * ### Targeting Cookies
  * ### Functional Cookies


#### Your Privacy
When you visit any website, it may store or retrieve information on your browser, mostly in the form of cookies. This information might be about you, your preferences or your device and is mostly used to make the site work as you expect it to. The information does not usually directly identify you, but it can give you a more personalized web experience. Because we respect your right to privacy, you can choose not to allow some types of cookies. From the list on left, please choose whether this site may use Performance and/or Targeting Cookies. By selecting Strictly Necessary Cookies only, you are requesting Cisco not to sell or share your personal data. Note, blocking some types of cookies may impact your experience on the site and the services we are able to offer.
#### Strictly Necessary Cookies
Always Active
These cookies are necessary for the website to function and cannot be switched off in our systems. They are usually only set in response to actions made by you which amount to a request for services, such as setting your privacy preferences, logging in or filling in forms. You can set your browser to block or alert you about these cookies, but some parts of the site will not then work. These cookies do not store any personally identifiable information.
Cookies Details
#### Performance Cookies
Performance Cookies
These cookies provide metrics related to the performance and usability of our site. They are primarily focused on gathering information about how you interact with our site, including: page load times, response times, error messages, and allowing a replay of a visitor’s interactions with our site, which enables us to review and analyze visitor behavior, helping to improve site usability and functionality. These cookies also allow us to count visits and traffic sources so we can measure and improve the performance of our site. They help us to know which pages are the most and least popular and see how visitors move around the site. If you do not allow these cookies we will not know when you have visited our site and will not be able to monitor its performance.
Cookies Details
#### Targeting Cookies
Targeting Cookies
These cookies may be set through our site by our advertising partners. They may be used by those companies to build a profile of your interests and show you relevant adverts on other sites. They do not store directly personal information, but are based on uniquely identifying your browser and internet device. If you do not allow these cookies, you will experience less targeted advertising.
Cookies Details
#### Functional Cookies
Functional Cookies
These cookies enable the website to provide enhanced functionality and personalisation. They may be set by us or by third party providers whose services we have added to our pages. If you do not allow these cookies then some or all of these services may not function properly.

Overview
EOL13770
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Meeting Server 1000 M5. The last day to order the affected product(s) is August 30, 2021 . Customers with active service contracts will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract.
The CMS 1000 M5 was based upon Intel chipsets that have been superseded. Therefore, the CMS 1000 M5 is being superseded by the CMS 1000 M5 v2 (CTI-CMS1KM52BUN-K9).
End-of-life milestones
**Table 1.** End-of-Life Milestones and Dates for the Cisco Meeting Server 1000 M5  
|  Milestone  |  Definition  |  Date  |  
| --- | --- | --- |  
|  **End-of-Life Announcement Date**  |  The date the document that announces the end-of-sale and  
end-of-life of a product is distributed to the general public.  |  March 1, 2021   |  
|  **End-of-Sale Date:  
HW **  |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  August 30, 2021   |  
|  **Last Ship Date:  
HW **  |  The last-possible ship date that can be requested of Cisco and/or its contract manufacturers. Actual ship date is dependent on lead time.  |  November 28, 2021   |  
|  **End of Vulnerability/Security Support:  
HW **  |  The last date that Cisco Engineering may release a planned maintenance release or scheduled software remedy for a security vulnerability issue.   |  August 29, 2024   |  
|  **End of Routine Failure Analysis Date:  
HW **  |  The last-possible date a routine failure analysis may be performed to determine the cause of hardware product failure or defect.  |  August 30, 2022   |  
|  **End of New Service Attachment Date:  
HW **  |  For equipment and software that is not covered by a service-and-support contract, this is the last date to order a new service-and-support contract or add the equipment and/or software to an existing service-and-support contract.  |  August 30, 2022   |  
|  **End of Service Contract Renewal Date:  
HW **  |  The last date to extend or renew a service contract for the product.  |  November 25, 2025   |  
|  **Last Date of Support:  
HW **  |  The last date to receive applicable service and support for the product as entitled by active service contracts or by warranty terms and conditions. After this date, all support services for the product are unavailable, and the product becomes obsolete.   |  August 31, 2026   |  
HW = Hardware OS SW = Operating System Software App. SW = Application Software 
Product part numbers

|  **CTI-CMS-1000-M5-K9**  |  CMS 1000 M5 Server  |  CTI-CMS1KM52BUN-K9  |  CMS 1000 M5v2 Server bundle  |  -  |  
|  **CIT3-CPU-6140**  |  2.3 GHz 6140/140W 18C/24.75MB Cache/DDR4 2666MHz  |  CTI-CMS1KM52BUN-K9  |  CMS 1000 M5v2 Server bundle  |  -  |  
|  **CIT3-MR-X16G1RS-H**  |  16GB DDR4-2666-MHz RDIMM/PC4-21300/single rank/x4/1.2v  |  CTI-CMS1KM52BUN-K9  |  CMS 1000 M5v2 Server bundle  |  -  |  
|  **CIT3-HD300G10K12N**  |  300GB 12G SAS 10K RPM SFF HDD  |  There is currently no replacement product available for this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
Product migration options
Customers may be able to use the Cisco Technology Migration Program (TMP) where applicable to trade-in eligible products and receive credit toward the purchase of new Cisco equipment. For more information about Cisco TMP, customers should work with their Cisco Partner or Cisco account team. Cisco Partners can find additional TMP information on Partner Central at <https://www.cisco.com/web/partners/incentives_and_promotions/tmp.html>. 
Customers may be able to continue to purchase the Cisco Meeting Server 1000 M5 through the Cisco Certified Refurbished Equipment program. Refurbished units may be available in limited supply for sale in certain countries on a first-come, first-served basis until the Last Date of Support has been reached. The program is open to all business users of Cisco equipment and its associated brands and subsidiaries. For more information, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/web/about/ac227/ac228/ac231/about_cisco_takeback_recycling.html>.

Overview
EOL13526
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Meeting Server 2.x software, Cisco Meeting Management 2.x software and Cisco Meeting App. The last day to order the affected product(s) is March 1, 2021. Customers with active service contracts will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract.
End-of-life milestones
**Table 1.** End-of-life milestones and dates for the Cisco Meeting Server 2.x software, Cisco Meeting Management 2.x software and Cisco Meeting App  
|  Milestone  |  Definition  |  Date   |  
| --- | --- | --- |  
|  **End-of-Life Announcement Date**  |  The date the document that announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life of a product is distributed to the general public.  |  August 31, 2020   |  
|  **End-of-Sale Date:  
App SW **  |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  March 1, 2021   |  
|  **Last Ship Date:  
App SW **  |  The last-possible ship date that can be requested of Cisco and/or its contract manufacturers. Actual ship date is dependent on lead time.  |  May 30, 2021   |  
|  **End of SW Maintenance Releases Date:  
App SW **  |  The last date that Cisco Engineering may release any final software maintenance releases or bug fixes. After this date, Cisco Engineering will no longer develop, repair, maintain, or test the product software.  |  March 1, 2022   |  
|  **End of New Service Attachment Date:  
App SW **  |  For equipment and software that is not covered by a service-and-support contract, this is the last date to order a new service-and-support contract or add the equipment and/or software to an existing service-and-support contract.  |  March 1, 2022   |  
|  **End of Service Contract Renewal Date:  
App SW **  |  The last date to extend or renew a service contract for the product.  |  May 28, 2023   |  
|  **Last Date of Support:  
App SW **  |  The last date to receive applicable service and support for the product as entitled by active service contracts or by warranty terms and conditions. After this date, all support services for the product are unavailable, and the product becomes obsolete.   |  February 29, 2024   |  
HW = Hardware OS SW = Operating System Software App. SW = Application Software 
Product part numbers
**Table 2.** Product part numbers affected by this announcement  
|  End-of-Sale Product Part Number  |  Product Description  |  Replacement Product Part Number  |  Replacement Product Description  |  Additional Information   |  
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |  
|  **SW-CMS-2X-K9**  |  Cisco Meeting Server (CMS) 2.x Software image  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
|  **CMS1K-SW-2X**  |  Cisco Meeting server 1000 sw preload  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
|  **CMS2K-SW-2X**  |  Cisco meeting server 2000 preload  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
Product migration options
Customers are advised to upgrade to the latest version of Cisco Meeting Server and Cisco Meeting Management, version 3.0 or later. Cisco Meeting App will no longer be supported from Cisco Meeting Server version 3.0 and later. Customers can use the Cisco Meeting Server web app to join and manage spaces via a supported webRTC capable internet browser. Alternatively, if customers need a "thick" app that is downloaded to the user's device then they should migrate to Cisco Jabber. 
Service prices for Cisco products are subject to change after the product End-of-Sale date.
The Cisco Takeback and Recycle program helps businesses properly dispose of surplus products that have reached their end of useful life. The program is open to all business users of Cisco equipment and its associated brands and subsidiaries. For more information, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/web/about/ac227/ac228/ac231/about_cisco_takeback_recycling.html>.
For more information
For more information about the Cisco End-of-Life Policy, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/en/US/products/products_end-of-life_policy.html>.
For more information about the Cisco Product Warranties, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/en/US/products/prod_warranties_listing.html>.
To subscribe to receive end-of-life/end-of-sale information, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/cisco/support/notifications.html>.

Overview
EOL13344
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Meeting Server (CMS) ACU License. The last day to order the affected product(s) is October 30, 2020. Customers with active service contracts will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract.
End-of-life milestones
**Table 1.** End-of-life milestones and dates for the Cisco Meeting Server (CMS) ACU License  
|  Milestone  |  Definition  |  Date   |  
| --- | --- | --- |  
|  **End-of-Life Announcement Date**  |  The date the document that announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life of a product is distributed to the general public.  |  May 1, 2020   |  
|  **End-of-Sale Date  
License **  |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  October 30, 2020   |  
|  **Last Ship Date  
License **  |  The last-possible ship date that can be requested of Cisco and/or its contract manufacturers. Actual ship date is dependent on lead time.  |  January 28, 2021   |  
Product part numbers
**Table 2.** Product part numbers affected by this announcement  
|  End-of-Sale Product Part Number  |  Product Description  |  Replacement Product Part Number  |  Replacement Product Description  |  Additional Information   |  
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |  
|  **LIC-CMS-ACU=**  |  Cisco Meeting Server (CMS) ACU License  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
Product migration options
Existing ACU licenses can be migrated to on premise perpetual shared multiparty licenses at a ratio of 2.5 ACU to one shared multiparty license (TP-SMP-SL2SMP). For new licenses you can choose between perpetual and subscription offers. Flexible cloud or on-premise subscription for Enterprise agreement, named user or active user licenses (A-FLEX). On-premise subscription for enterprise agreement, personal multiparty or shared multiparty (A-CMS). On-premise perpetual personal multiparty licenses (under CUWL-12X-K9) or shared multiparty licenses (TP-SMP-K9). Contact your Cisco sales representative for more information. 
Service prices for Cisco products are subject to change after the product End-of-Sale date.
The Cisco Takeback and Recycle program helps businesses properly dispose of surplus products that have reached their end of useful life. The program is open to all business users of Cisco equipment and its associated brands and subsidiaries. For more information, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/web/about/ac227/ac228/ac231/about_cisco_takeback_recycling.html>.
For more information
For more information about the Cisco End-of-Life Policy, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/en/US/products/products_end-of-life_policy.html>.
For more information about the Cisco Product Warranties, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/en/US/products/prod_warranties_listing.html>.
To subscribe to receive end-of-life/end-of-sale information, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/cisco/support/notifications.html>.

Overview
EOL13190 - Amended
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Vyopta vAnalytics. The last day to order the affected product(s) is April 3, 2020. The last day to renew or add to an existing subscription is April 3, 2020. Customers with active service contracts and subscriptions (as applicable) will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts and subscriptions (as applicable), support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract and subscription. 
End-of-life milestones

|  Milestone  |  Definition  |  Date   |  
| --- | --- | --- |

|  **End-of-Sale Date***  |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  April 3, 2020   |  
|  **End of Change/Renewal Date* Subscription**  |  The last date to Renew or Add to an existing subscription.  |  April 3, 2020   |  
|  **Last Date of Support** Subscription**  |  The last date to receive applicable subscription entitlements, service and support for the product as entitled by active subscriptions and service contracts(as applicable) or by warranty terms and conditions. After this date, all subscription and support services for the product are unavailable, and the product becomes obsolete.   |  April 30, 2023   |  
Product part numbers

| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |  
|  **V-CLOUD**  |  SolutionsPlus: Vyopta vAnalytics Cloud Starter Pack Sub  |  C-CPM  |  SolutionsPlus: Vyopta CPM Cloud Subscription  |  -  |  
|  **V-CLOUD-SP**  |  Vyopta vAnalytics Cloud Starter Pack Subscription  |  There is currently no replacement product available for this product.  |  -  |  -  |

Bias-Free Language
### Bias-Free Language
The documentation set for this product strives to use bias-free language. For the purposes of this documentation set, bias-free is defined as language that does not imply discrimination based on age, disability, gender, racial identity, ethnic identity, sexual orientation, socioeconomic status, and intersectionality. Exceptions may be present in the documentation due to language that is hardcoded in the user interfaces of the product software, language used based on RFP documentation, or language that is used by a referenced third-party product. [Learn more](https://www.cisco.com/c/en/us/about/social-justice/inclusive-language-policy.html) about how Cisco is using Inclusive Language.
EOL13029
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Telepresence as a Service (TPaaS). The last day to order the affected product(s) is December 14, 2019. The last day to renew or add to an existing subscription is December 14, 2019. Customers with active service contracts and subscriptions (as applicable) will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts and subscriptions (as applicable), support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract and subscription. 
**Table 1.** End-of-Life Milestones and Dates for the Cisco Telepresence as a Service (TPaaS)  
|  Milestone  |  Definition  |  Date   |  
| --- | --- | --- |  
|  End-of-Life Announcement Date   |  The date the document that announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life of a product is distributed to the general public.  |  June 15, 2019   |  
|  End-of-Sale Date*  |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  December 14, 2019   |  
|  End of Change/Renewal Date*   
Subscription   |  The last date to Renew or Add to an existing subscription.  |  December 14, 2019   |  
|  Last Date of Support**   
Subscription   |  The last date to receive applicable subscription entitlements, service and support for the product as entitled by active subscriptions and service contracts(as applicable) or by warranty terms and conditions. After this date, all subscription and support services for the product are unavailable, and the product becomes obsolete.   |  December 31, 2022   |  
**Table 2.** Product Part Numbers Affected by This Announcement  
|  End-of-Sale Product Part Number  |  Product Description  |  Replacement Product Part Number  |  Replacement Product Description  |  Additional Information   |  
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |  
|  A-TPAAS  |  TP as a Service subscription for CMS  |  A-CMS  |  Cisco Meeting Server Subscription  |  -  |  
|  A-TPAAS-PAK  |  TP as a Service PAK  |  A-FLEX-CMS-PAK  |  CMS Product Authorization Key (1)  |  -  |  
|  A-TPAAS-PMPPLUS  |  TP as a Service - Personal Multi Party for CMS  |  A-CMS-PMP  |  CMS On-Prem Named User (PMP) Host License  |  -  |  
|  A-TPAAS-SMPPLUS  |  TP as a Service - Shared Multi Party for CMS  |  A-CMS-SMP  |  CMS On-Prem Shared Meeting License  |  -  |  
Product Migration Options
Service prices for Cisco products are subject to change after the product End-of-Sale date.
The Cisco Takeback and Recycle program helps businesses properly dispose of surplus products that have reached their end of useful life. The program is open to all business users of Cisco equipment and its associated brands and subsidiaries. For more information, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/web/about/ac227/ac228/ac231/about_cisco_takeback_recycling.html>.
For More Information
For more information about the Cisco End-of-Life Policy, go to:  
<https://www.cisco.com/en/US/products/products_end-of-life_policy.html>
For more information about the Cisco Product Warranties, go to:  
<https://www.cisco.com/en/US/products/prod_warranties_listing.html>
To subscribe to receive end-of-life/end-of-sale information, go to:  
<https://www.cisco.com/cisco/support/notifications.html>

EOL12882 - Amended
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Meeting Server Branding Options. The last day to order the affected product(s) is August 2, 2019. Customers with active service contracts will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract. 
Branding is now included without needing a license with Cisco Meeting Server 2.4 or higher. 
**Table 1.** End-of-Life Milestones and Dates for the Cisco Meeting Server Branding Options  
|  Milestone  |  Definition  |  Date   |  
| --- | --- | --- |  
|  End-of-Life Announcement Date   |  The date the document that announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life of a product is distributed to the general public.  |  February 1, 2019   |  
|  End-of-Sale Date   
License   |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  August 2, 2019   |  
|  Last Ship Date   
License   |  The last-possible ship date that can be requested of Cisco and/or its contract manufacturers. Actual ship date is dependent on lead time.  |  October 31, 2019   |  
**Table 2.** Product Part Numbers Affected by This Announcement  
|  End-of-Sale Product Part Number  |  Product Description  |  Replacement Product Part Number  |  Replacement Product Description  |  Additional Information   |  
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |  
|  CMS-BRANDING  |  Cisco Meeting Server (CMS) Branding  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
|  LIC-CMS-MBRD  |  Cisco Meeting Server (CMS) System branding (Multiple Brand)  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
|  LIC-CMS-MBRD-EA  |  Cisco Meeting Server (CMS) System Branding - Multi Brand  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
|  LIC-CMS-SBRD  |  Cisco Meeting Server (CMS) System branding (Single Brand)  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
|  LIC-CMS-SBRD-EA  |  Cisco Meeting Server (CMS) System Branding - Single Brand  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
Product Migration Options
Cisco Meeting Server version 2.4 or higher does not require Branding license for use of Branding features. Customers wanting to use branding with Cisco Meeting Server just need to use version 2.4 or higher. 
Service prices for Cisco products are subject to change after the product End-of-Sale date.

#### Your Privacy
When you visit any website, it may store or retrieve information on your browser, mostly in the form of cookies. This information might be about you, your preferences or your device and is mostly used to make the site work as you expect it to. The information does not usually directly identify you, but it can give you a more personalized web experience. Because we respect your right to privacy, you can choose not to allow some types of cookies. From the list on left, please choose whether this site may use Performance and/or Targeting Cookies. By selecting Strictly Necessary Cookies only, you are requesting Cisco not to sell or share your personal data. Note, blocking some types of cookies may impact your experience on the site and the services we are able to offer.
#### Strictly Necessary Cookies
Always Active
These cookies are necessary for the website to function and cannot be switched off in our systems. They are usually only set in response to actions made by you which amount to a request for services, such as setting your privacy preferences, logging in or filling in forms. You can set your browser to block or alert you about these cookies, but some parts of the site will not then work. These cookies do not store any personally identifiable information.
Cookies Details
#### Performance Cookies
Performance Cookies
These cookies provide metrics related to the performance and usability of our site. They are primarily focused on gathering information about how you interact with our site, including: page load times, response times, error messages, and allowing a replay of a visitor’s interactions with our site, which enables us to review and analyze visitor behavior, helping to improve site usability and functionality. These cookies also allow us to count visits and traffic sources so we can measure and improve the performance of our site. They help us to know which pages are the most and least popular and see how visitors move around the site. If you do not allow these cookies we will not know when you have visited our site and will not be able to monitor its performance.
Cookies Details
#### Targeting Cookies
Targeting Cookies
These cookies may be set through our site by our advertising partners. They may be used by those companies to build a profile of your interests and show you relevant adverts on other sites. They do not store directly personal information, but are based on uniquely identifying your browser and internet device. If you do not allow these cookies, you will experience less targeted advertising.
Cookies Details
#### Functional Cookies
Functional Cookies
These cookies enable the website to provide enhanced functionality and personalisation. They may be set by us or by third party providers whose services we have added to our pages. If you do not allow these cookies then some or all of these services may not function properly.
Cookies Details

EOL12677 - Amended
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Meeting Server 2000. The last day to order the affected product(s) is February 18, 2019. Customers with active service contracts will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract.
**Table 1.** End-of-Life Milestones and Dates for the Cisco Meeting Server 2000  
|  Milestone  |  Definition  |  Date   |  
| --- | --- | --- |  
|  End-of-Life Announcement Date   |  The date the document that announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life of a product is distributed to the general public.  |  November 19, 2018  |  
|  End-of-Sale Date:  
HW   |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  February 18, 2019  |  
|  Last Ship Date:  
HW   |  The last-possible ship date that can be requested of Cisco and/or its contract manufacturers. Actual ship date is dependent on lead time.  |  May 19, 2019  |  
|  End of SW Maintenance Releases Date:  
HW   |  The last date that Cisco Engineering may release any final software maintenance releases or bug fixes. After this date, Cisco Engineering will no longer develop, repair, maintain, or test the product software.  |  February 18, 2020  |  
|  End of Vulnerability/Security Support:  
HW   |  The last date that Cisco Engineering may release a planned maintenance release or scheduled software remedy for a security vulnerability issue.  |  February 17, 2022  |  
|  End of Routine Failure Analysis Date:   
HW   |  The last-possible date a routine failure analysis may be performed to determine the cause of hardware product failure or defect.  |  February 18, 2020  |  
|  End of New Service Attachment Date:  
HW   |  For equipment and software that is not covered by a service-and-support contract, this is the last date to order a new service-and-support contract or add the equipment and/or software to an existing service-and-support contract.  |  February 18, 2020  |  
|  End of Service Contract Renewal Date:   
HW   |  The last date to extend or renew a service contract for the product.  |  May 16, 2023  |  
|  Last Date of Support:  
HW   |  The last date to receive applicable service and support for the product as entitled by active service contracts or by warranty terms and conditions. After this date, all support services for the product are unavailable, and the product becomes obsolete.   |  February 29, 2024  |  
HW = Hardware OS SW = Operating System Software App. SW = Application Software 
**Table 2.** Product Part Numbers Affected by This Announcement  
|  End-of-Sale Product Part Number  |  Product Description  |  Replacement Product Part Number  |  Replacement Product Description  |  Additional Information  |  
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |  
|  CIT3-B200-M4  |  UCS B200 M4 w/o CPU, mem, drive bays, HDD, mezz  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
|  CIT3-B200-M4-CON  |  UCS B200 M4 w/o CPU, mem, drive bays, HDD, mezz  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
|  CTI-CMS-2000-K9  |  Cisco Meeting Server 2000  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
|  CTI-CMS-2K-BUN-K9  |  Cisco Meeting Server 2000 Bundle  |  See the Product Migration Options section below for detailed information on replacing this product.  |  -  |  -  |  
Product Migration Options
The Cisco Meeting Server 2000 (M4 version) is being superseded by the Cisco Meeting server 2000 (M5 version). The M5 version will be available for ordering by February 2019 using the part number CTI-CMS2KM5-BUN-K9.

Bias-Free Language
### Bias-Free Language
The documentation set for this product strives to use bias-free language. For the purposes of this documentation set, bias-free is defined as language that does not imply discrimination based on age, disability, gender, racial identity, ethnic identity, sexual orientation, socioeconomic status, and intersectionality. Exceptions may be present in the documentation due to language that is hardcoded in the user interfaces of the product software, language used based on RFP documentation, or language that is used by a referenced third-party product. [Learn more](https://www.cisco.com/c/en/us/about/social-justice/inclusive-language-policy.html) about how Cisco is using Inclusive Language.
EOL12676 - Amended
Cisco announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life dates for the Cisco Meeting Server 1000. The last day to order the affected product(s) is March 1, 2019. Customers with active service contracts will continue to receive support from the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) as shown in Table 1 of the EoL bulletin. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract. 
The Original CMS 1000 was based upon a UCS C220 M4 server which has been superseded by the UCS C220 M5 server. Therefore, the CMS 1000 is being superseded by the CMS 1000 M5 (CTI-CMS1KM5-BUN-K9).
**Table 1.** End-of-Life Milestones and Dates for the Cisco Meeting Server 1000  
|  Milestone  |  Definition  |  Date  |  
| --- | --- | --- |  
|  End-of-Life Announcement Date   |  The date the document that announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life of a product is distributed to the general public.  |  August 31, 2018  |  
|  End-of-Sale Date:  
HW   |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  March 1, 2019  |  
|  Last Ship Date:  
HW   |  The last-possible ship date that can be requested of Cisco and/or its contract manufacturers. Actual ship date is dependent on lead time.  |  May 30, 2019  |  
|  End of SW Maintenance Releases Date:  
HW   |  The last date that Cisco Engineering may release any final software maintenance releases or bug fixes. After this date, Cisco Engineering will no longer develop, repair, maintain, or test the product software.  |  February 29, 2020  |  
|  End of Vulnerability/Security Support:  
HW   |  The last date that Cisco Engineering may release a planned maintenance release or scheduled software remedy for a security vulnerability issue.   |  February 28, 2022  |  
|  End of Routine Failure Analysis Date:   
HW   |  The last-possible date a routine failure analysis may be performed to determine the cause of hardware product failure or defect.  |  February 29, 2020  |  
|  End of New Service Attachment Date:  
HW   |  For equipment and software that is not covered by a service-and-support contract, this is the last date to order a new service-and-support contract or add the equipment and/or software to an existing service-and-support contract.  |  February 29, 2020  |  
|  End of Service Contract Renewal Date:   
HW   |  The last date to extend or renew a service contract for the product.  |  May 27, 2023  |  
|  Last Date of Support:  
HW   |  The last date to receive applicable service and support for the product as entitled by active service contracts or by warranty terms and conditions. After this date, all support services for the product are unavailable, and the product becomes obsolete.   |  February 29, 2024  |  
HW = Hardware OS SW = Operating System Software App. SW = Application Software 
**Table 2.** Product Part Numbers Affected by This Announcement  
|  End-of-Sale Product Part Number  |  Product Description  |  Replacement Product Part Number  |  Replacement Product Description  |  Additional Information   |  
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |  
|  CIT3-CPU-E52695E  |  2.10 GHz E5-2695 v4/120W 18C/45MB Cache/DDR4 2400MHz  |  CTI-CMS1KM5-BUN-K9  |  Cisco Meeting Server 1000 M5 Bundle  |  -  |  
|  CIT3-MR-1X081RV-A  |  8GB DDR4-2400-MHz RDIMM/PC4-19200/single rank/x4/1.2v  |  CTI-CMS1KM5-BUN-K9  |  Cisco Meeting Server 1000 M5 Bundle  |  -  |  
|  CIT3-MRAID12G  |  Cisco 12G SAS Modular Raid Controller  |  CTI-CMS1KM5-BUN-K9  |  Cisco Meeting Server 1000 M5 Bundle  |  -  |  
|  CTI-CMS-1000-K9  |  Cisco Meeting Server 1000  |  CTI-CMS1KM5-BUN-K9  |  Cisco Meeting Server 1000 M5 Bundle  |  -  |  
|  CTI-CMS-1K-BUN-K9  |  Cisco Meeting Server 1000 Bundle  |  CTI-CMS1KM5-BUN-K9  |  Cisco Meeting Server 1000 M5 Bundle  |  -  |  
Product Migration Options
Customers may be able to use the Cisco Technology Migration Program (TMP) where applicable to trade-in eligible products and receive credit toward the purchase of new Cisco equipment. For more information about Cisco TMP, customers should work with their Cisco Partner or Cisco account team. Cisco Partners can find additional TMP information on Partner Central at: <https://www.cisco.com/web/partners/incentives_and_promotions/tmp.html>.
Customers may be able to continue to purchase the Cisco Meeting Server 1000 through the Cisco Certified Refurbished Equipment program. Refurbished units may be available in limited supply for sale in certain countries on a first-come, first-served basis until the Last Date of Support has been reached. For information about the Cisco Certified Refurbished Equipment program, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/go/eos>.
Service prices for Cisco products are subject to change after the product End-of-Sale date.
The Cisco Takeback and Recycle program helps businesses properly dispose of surplus products that have reached their end of useful life. The program is open to all business users of Cisco equipment and its associated brands and subsidiaries. For more information, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/web/about/ac227/ac228/ac231/about_cisco_takeback_recycling.html>.
For More Information
For more information about the Cisco End-of-Life Policy, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/en/US/products/products_end-of-life_policy.html>.
For more information about the Cisco Product Warranties, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/en/US/products/prod_warranties_listing.html>.
To subscribe to receive end-of-life/end-of-sale information, go to: <https://www.cisco.com/cisco/support/notifications.html>.
Any authorized translation issued by Cisco Systems or affiliates of this end-of-life Product Bulletin is intended to help customers understand the content described in the English version. This translation is the result of a commercially reasonable effort; however, if there are discrepancies between the English version and the translated document, please refer to the English version, which is considered authoritative.

## Consent Manager
  * ### Your Privacy
  * ### Strictly Necessary Cookies
  * ### Performance Cookies
  * ### Targeting Cookies

#### Your Privacy
When you visit any website, it may store or retrieve information on your browser, mostly in the form of cookies. This information might be about you, your preferences or your device and is mostly used to make the site work as you expect it to. The information does not usually directly identify you, but it can give you a more personalized web experience. Because we respect your right to privacy, you can choose not to allow some types of cookies. From the list on left, please choose whether this site may use Performance and/or Targeting Cookies. By selecting Strictly Necessary Cookies only, you are requesting Cisco not to sell or share your personal data. Note, blocking some types of cookies may impact your experience on the site and the services we are able to offer.
#### Strictly Necessary Cookies
Always Active
These cookies are necessary for the website to function and cannot be switched off in our systems. They are usually only set in response to actions made by you which amount to a request for services, such as setting your privacy preferences, logging in or filling in forms. You can set your browser to block or alert you about these cookies, but some parts of the site will not then work. These cookies do not store any personally identifiable information.
Cookies Details
#### Performance Cookies
Performance Cookies
These cookies provide metrics related to the performance and usability of our site. They are primarily focused on gathering information about how you interact with our site, including: page load times, response times, error messages, and allowing a replay of a visitor’s interactions with our site, which enables us to review and analyze visitor behavior, helping to improve site usability and functionality. These cookies also allow us to count visits and traffic sources so we can measure and improve the performance of our site. They help us to know which pages are the most and least popular and see how visitors move around the site. If you do not allow these cookies we will not know when you have visited our site and will not be able to monitor its performance.
Cookies Details
#### Targeting Cookies
Targeting Cookies
These cookies may be set through our site by our advertising partners. They may be used by those companies to build a profile of your interests and show you relevant adverts on other sites. They do not store directly personal information, but are based on uniquely identifying your browser and internet device. If you do not allow these cookies, you will experience less targeted advertising.
Cookies Details
#### Functional Cookies
Functional Cookies
These cookies enable the website to provide enhanced functionality and personalisation. They may be set by us or by third party providers whose services we have added to our pages. If you do not allow these cookies then some or all of these services may not function properly.

EOL12691 - Amended
Cisco announces the end-of-sale dates for the Cisco Meeting Server Migration Program. The last day to order the affected product(s) is November 5, 2018. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract.
Cisco announces the end-of-sale dates for the Cisco TelePresence Server or Cisco MCU to Cisco Meeting Server migration. The last day to order the affected product(s) is November 5, 2018. Cisco Meeting Server is not end-of-sale or support with this notice. This notice is only for end-of-sale of the migration pricing program. Customers who purchased Cisco Meeting Server using this migration program will continue to renew services and access support per existing support contracts.
**Table 1.** End-of-Life Milestones and Dates for the Cisco Meeting Server Migration Program  
|  Milestone  |  Definition  |  Date  |  
| --- | --- | --- |  
|  End-of-Life Announcement Date   |  The date the document that announces the end-of-sale and end-of-life of a product is distributed to the general public.  |  August 6, 2018  |  
|  End-of-Sale Date   
License   |  The last date to order the product through Cisco point-of-sale mechanisms. The product is no longer for sale after this date.  |  November 5, 2018  |  
|  Last Ship Date   
License   |  The last-possible ship date that can be requested of Cisco and/or its contract manufacturers. Actual ship date is dependent on lead time.  |  February 3, 2019  |  
**Table 2.** Product Part Numbers Affected by This Announcement  
|  End-of-Sale Product Part Number  |  Product Description  |  Replacement Product Part Number  |  Replacement Product Description  |  Additional Information   |  
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |  
|  LIC-MCU45-4PL2SMP+  |  MCU 4PL to SMP PLUS Migration (MCU 4200/4500)  |  See Product Migration Options section for details.  |  Cisco Meeting Server  |  -  |  
|  LIC-SMP-2-CMS  |  Existing SMP to SMP+ with CMS (Cisco Meeting Server)  |  See Product Migration Options section for details.  |  Cisco Meeting Server  |  -  |  
|  LIC-SMP-EP-2-CMS  |  Existing SMP-EP to CMS (Cisco Meeting Server)  |  See Product Migration Options section for details.  |  Cisco Meeting Server  |  -  |  
|  LIC-TSORMCU-2-SMP+  |  SL or MCU4PL to SMP PLUS Migration(MCU 8510/8420/5300/TS300)  |  See Product Migration Options section for details.  |  Cisco Meeting Server  |  -  |  
Product Migration Options
Cisco Meeting Server continues to be available using Flex Licensing, Personal Multiparty with CUWL Meetings, Shared Multiparty, and ACU. For details please visit the product pages <https://www.cisco.com/go/meetingserver> and choose under partner resources the video conferencing ordering guide. 
Service prices for Cisco products are subject to change after the product End-of-Sale date.
The Cisco Takeback and Recycle program helps businesses properly dispose of surplus products that have reached their end of useful life. The program is open to all business users of Cisco equipment and its associated brands and subsidiaries. Cisco announces the end-of-sale dates for the Cisco TCS to CMS Pricing Promotion. The last day to order the affected product(s) is November 5, 2018. Table 1 describes the end-of-life milestones, definitions, and dates for the affected product(s). Table 2 lists the product part numbers affected by this announcement. For customers with active and paid service and support contracts, support will be available under the terms and conditions of customers' service contract. 
Cisco announces the end-of-sale dates for the Cisco TelePresence Content Server (TCS) to Cisco Meeting Server recording ports. The last day to order the affected product(s) is September 31, 2018. Cisco Meeting Server is not end-of-sale or support with this notice. This notice is only for end-of-sale of the TCS to CMS Recording ports migration pricing program. Customers who purchased Cisco Meeting Server using this migration program will continue to renew services and access support per existing support contracts.

#### Your Privacy
When you visit any website, it may store or retrieve information on your browser, mostly in the form of cookies. This information might be about you, your preferences or your device and is mostly used to make the site work as you expect it to. The information does not usually directly identify you, but it can give you a more personalized web experience. Because we respect your right to privacy, you can choose not to allow some types of cookies. From the list on left, please choose whether this site may use Performance and/or Targeting Cookies. By selecting Strictly Necessary Cookies only, you are requesting Cisco not to sell or share your personal data. Note, blocking some types of cookies may impact your experience on the site and the services we are able to offer.
#### Strictly Necessary Cookies
Always Active
These cookies are necessary for the website to function and cannot be switched off in our systems. They are usually only set in response to actions made by you which amount to a request for services, such as setting your privacy preferences, logging in or filling in forms. You can set your browser to block or alert you about these cookies, but some parts of the site will not then work. These cookies do not store any personally identifiable information.
Cookies Details
#### Performance Cookies
Performance Cookies
These cookies provide metrics related to the performance and usability of our site. They are primarily focused on gathering information about how you interact with our site, including: page load times, response times, error messages, and allowing a replay of a visitor’s interactions with our site, which enables us to review and analyze visitor behavior, helping to improve site usability and functionality. These cookies also allow us to count visits and traffic sources so we can measure and improve the performance of our site. They help us to know which pages are the most and least popular and see how visitors move around the site. If you do not allow these cookies we will not know when you have visited our site and will not be able to monitor its performance.
Cookies Details
#### Targeting Cookies
Targeting Cookies
These cookies may be set through our site by our advertising partners. They may be used by those companies to build a profile of your interests and show you relevant adverts on other sites. They do not store directly personal information, but are based on uniquely identifying your browser and internet device. If you do not allow these cookies, you will experience less targeted advertising.
Cookies Details
#### Functional Cookies
Functional Cookies
These cookies enable the website to provide enhanced functionality and personalisation. They may be set by us or by third party providers whose services we have added to our pages. If you do not allow these cookies then some or all of these services may not function properly.

# Cisco Meeting Server Web Bridge Denial of Service Vulnerability
Medium
Advisory ID: 
cisco-sa-cms-segfault-G6ES4Ve8
First Published:
2023 November 1 16:00 GMT
Version 1.0: 
[Final](https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/resources/security_vulnerability_policy.html#final)
Workarounds: 
No workarounds available

[CSCwc01047](https://bst.cloudapps.cisco.com/bugsearch/bug/CSCwc01047)

Email 
## 
Summary 
  * A vulnerability in an API of the Web Bridge feature of Cisco Meeting Server could allow an unauthenticated, remote attacker to cause a denial of service (DoS) condition.
This vulnerability is due to insufficient validation of HTTP requests. An attacker could exploit this vulnerability by sending crafted HTTP packets to an affected device. A successful exploit could allow the attacker to cause a partial availability condition, which could cause ongoing video calls to be dropped due to the invalid packets reaching the Web Bridge.
Cisco has released software updates that address this vulnerability. There are no workarounds that address this vulnerability.
This advisory is available at the following link:  
<https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/content/CiscoSecurityAdvisory/cisco-sa-cms-segfault-G6ES4Ve8>


## 
Affected Products 
  * ##  Vulnerable Products 
At the time of publication, this vulnerability affected Cisco Meeting Server.
For information about which Cisco software releases were vulnerable at the time of publication, see the [Fixed Software](https://www.cisco.com/c/en/us/support/docs/csa/cisco-sa-cms-segfault-G6ES4Ve8.html#fs) section of this advisory. See the Details section in the bug ID(s) at the top of this advisory for the most complete and current information.
##  Products Confirmed Not Vulnerable 
Only products listed in the [Vulnerable Products](https://www.cisco.com/c/en/us/support/docs/csa/cisco-sa-cms-segfault-G6ES4Ve8.html#vp) section of this advisory are known to be affected by this vulnerability.


## 
Workarounds 
  * There are no workarounds that address this vulnerability.


## 
Fixed Software 
  * When [considering software upgrades](https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/resources/security_vulnerability_policy.html#fixes), customers are advised to regularly consult the advisories for Cisco products, which are available from the [Cisco Security Advisories page](https://www.cisco.com/go/psirt), to determine exposure and a complete upgrade solution.
In all cases, customers should ensure that the devices to be upgraded contain sufficient memory and confirm that current hardware and software configurations will continue to be supported properly by the new release. If the information is not clear, customers are advised to contact the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) or their contracted maintenance providers.
### Fixed Releases
At the time of publication, the release information in the following table was accurate. See the Details section in the bug ID(s) at the top of this advisory for the most complete and current information.
The left column lists Cisco software releases, and the right column indicates whether a release was affected by the vulnerability that is described in this advisory and which release included the fix for this vulnerability.  
| Cisco Meeting Server Release  | First Fixed Release  |  
| --- | --- |  
| 3.5 and earlier  | Migrate to fixed release.  |  
| 3.6  | 3.6.1  |  
| 3.7  | Not vulnerable.  |  
| 3.8  | Not vulnerable.  |  
The Cisco Product Security Incident Response Team (PSIRT) validates only the affected and fixed release information that is documented in this advisory.


## 
Exploitation and Public Announcements 
  * The Cisco PSIRT is not aware of any public announcements or malicious use of the vulnerability that is described in this advisory.


## 
Source 
  * This vulnerability was found during the resolution of a Cisco TAC support case.


## 
Cisco Security Vulnerability Policy 
  * To learn about Cisco security vulnerability disclosure policies and publications, see the [Security Vulnerability Policy](http://www.cisco.com/web/about/security/psirt/security_vulnerability_policy.html). This document also contains instructions for obtaining fixed software and receiving security vulnerability information from Cisco.

## 
Summary 
  * A vulnerability in an API of the Call Bridge feature of Cisco Meeting Server could allow an unauthenticated, remote attacker to cause a denial of service (DoS) condition.
This vulnerability is due to improper handling of large series of message requests. An attacker could exploit this vulnerability by sending a series of messages to the vulnerable API. A successful exploit could allow the attacker to cause the affected device to reload, dropping all ongoing calls and resulting in a DoS condition.
Cisco has released software updates that address this vulnerability. There are no workarounds that address this vulnerability.
This advisory is available at the following link:  
<https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/content/CiscoSecurityAdvisory/cisco-sa-cms-LAHe8z5v>


## 
Affected Products 
  * ##  Vulnerable Products 
At the time of publication, this vulnerability affected Cisco Meeting Server.
For information about which Cisco software releases were vulnerable at the time of publication, see the [Fixed Software](https://www.cisco.com/c/en/us/support/docs/csa/cisco-sa-cms-LAHe8z5v.html#fs) section of this advisory. See the Details section in the bug ID(s) at the top of this advisory for the most complete and current information.
##  Products Confirmed Not Vulnerable 
Only products listed in the [Vulnerable Products](https://www.cisco.com/c/en/us/support/docs/csa/cisco-sa-cms-LAHe8z5v.html#vp) section of this advisory are known to be affected by this vulnerability.


## 
Workarounds 
  * There are no workarounds that address this vulnerability.


## 
Fixed Software 
  * When [considering software upgrades](https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/resources/security_vulnerability_policy.html#fixes), customers are advised to regularly consult the advisories for Cisco products, which are available from the [Cisco Security Advisories page](https://www.cisco.com/go/psirt), to determine exposure and a complete upgrade solution.
In all cases, customers should ensure that the devices to be upgraded contain sufficient memory and confirm that current hardware and software configurations will continue to be supported properly by the new release. If the information is not clear, customers are advised to contact the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) or their contracted maintenance providers.
### Fixed Releases
At the time of publication, the release information in the following table(s) was accurate. See the Details section in the bug ID(s) at the top of this advisory for the most complete and current information.
The left column lists Cisco software releases, and the right column indicates whether a release was affected by the vulnerability described in this advisory and which release included the fix for this vulnerability.  
| Cisco Meeting Server Release  | First Fixed Release  |  
| --- | --- |  
| 3.1 and earlier  | Not affected.  |  
| 3.2  | Migrate to a fixed release.  |  
| 3.3  | Not affected.  |  
The Cisco Product Security Incident Response Team (PSIRT) validates only the affected and fixed release information that is documented in this advisory.


## 
Exploitation and Public Announcements 
  * The Cisco PSIRT is not aware of any public announcements or malicious use of the vulnerability that is described in this advisory.


## 
Source 
  * This vulnerability was found during internal security testing.

#### Your Privacy
When you visit any website, it may store or retrieve information on your browser, mostly in the form of cookies. This information might be about you, your preferences or your device and is mostly used to make the site work as you expect it to. The information does not usually directly identify you, but it can give you a more personalized web experience. Because we respect your right to privacy, you can choose not to allow some types of cookies. From the list on left, please choose whether this site may use Performance and/or Targeting Cookies. By selecting Strictly Necessary Cookies only, you are requesting Cisco not to sell or share your personal data. Note, blocking some types of cookies may impact your experience on the site and the services we are able to offer.
#### Strictly Necessary Cookies
Always Active
These cookies are necessary for the website to function and cannot be switched off in our systems. They are usually only set in response to actions made by you which amount to a request for services, such as setting your privacy preferences, logging in or filling in forms. You can set your browser to block or alert you about these cookies, but some parts of the site will not then work. These cookies do not store any personally identifiable information.
Cookies Details
#### Performance Cookies
Performance Cookies
These cookies provide metrics related to the performance and usability of our site. They are primarily focused on gathering information about how you interact with our site, including: page load times, response times, error messages, and allowing a replay of a visitor’s interactions with our site, which enables us to review and analyze visitor behavior, helping to improve site usability and functionality. These cookies also allow us to count visits and traffic sources so we can measure and improve the performance of our site. They help us to know which pages are the most and least popular and see how visitors move around the site. If you do not allow these cookies we will not know when you have visited our site and will not be able to monitor its performance.
Cookies Details
#### Targeting Cookies
Targeting Cookies
These cookies may be set through our site by our advertising partners. They may be used by those companies to build a profile of your interests and show you relevant adverts on other sites. They do not store directly personal information, but are based on uniquely identifying your browser and internet device. If you do not allow these cookies, you will experience less targeted advertising.
Cookies Details
#### Functional Cookies
Functional Cookies
These cookies enable the website to provide enhanced functionality and personalisation. They may be set by us or by third party providers whose services we have added to our pages. If you do not allow these cookies then some or all of these services may not function properly.

# Cisco Meeting Server Extensible Messaging and Presence Protocol Denial of Service Vulnerability
Medium
Advisory ID: 
cisco-sa-cms-xmpp-dos-ptfGUsBx
First Published:
2020 February 19 16:00 GMT
Version 1.0: 
[Final](https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/resources/security_vulnerability_policy.html#final)
Workarounds: 
No workarounds available

[CSCvk42193](https://bst.cloudapps.cisco.com/bugsearch/bug/CSCvk42193)

Email 
## 
Summary 
  * A vulnerability in the Extensible Messaging and Presence Protocol (XMPP) feature of Cisco Meeting Server software could allow an unauthenticated, remote attacker to cause a denial of service (DoS) condition for users of XMPP conferencing applications. Other applications and processes are unaffected.
The vulnerability is due to improper input validation of XMPP packets. An attacker could exploit this vulnerability by sending crafted XMPP packets to an affected device. An exploit could allow the attacker to cause process crashes and a DoS condition for XMPP conferencing applications.
Cisco has released software updates that address the vulnerability described in this advisory. There are no workarounds that address this vulnerability.
This advisory is available at the following link:  
<https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/content/CiscoSecurityAdvisory/cisco-sa-cms-xmpp-dos-ptfGUsBx>


## 
Affected Products 
  * ##  Vulnerable Products 
At the time of publication, this vulnerability affected Cisco Meeting Server deployments that are running a software release earlier than Release 2.8 and that have the XMPP feature enabled.
See the Details section in the bug ID(s) at the top of this advisory for the most complete and current information.
**Note:** XMPP is not enabled by default.
### Determine the Status of XMPP for Cisco Meeting Server Deployments
Administrators can determine whether the XMPP feature is enabled on a device by using the **xmpp status** command in the device CLI, as shown in the following example:
> 
```
cms> xmpp status  
> **Enabled**                 : **true**  
> Clustered               : false
.
.
.

```

##  Products Confirmed Not Vulnerable 
Only products listed in the [Vulnerable Products](https://www.cisco.com/c/en/us/support/docs/csa/cisco-sa-cms-xmpp-dos-ptfGUsBx.html#vp) section of this advisory are known to be affected by this vulnerability.


## 
Workarounds 
  * There are no workarounds that address this vulnerability.


## 
Fixed Software 
  * When [considering software upgrades](https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/resources/security_vulnerability_policy.html#fixes), customers are advised to regularly consult the advisories for Cisco products, which are available from the [Cisco Security Advisories and Alerts page](https://www.cisco.com/go/psirt), to determine exposure and a complete upgrade solution.
In all cases, customers should ensure that the devices to be upgraded contain sufficient memory and confirm that current hardware and software configurations will continue to be supported properly by the new release. If the information is not clear, customers are advised to contact the Cisco Technical Assistance Center (TAC) or their contracted maintenance providers.
### Fixed Releases
At the time of publication, Cisco Meeting Server software releases 2.8 and later contained the fix for this vulnerability.
See the Details section in the bug ID(s) at the top of this advisory for the most complete and current information.


## 
Exploitation and Public Announcements 
  * The Cisco Product Security Incident Response Team (PSIRT) is not aware of any public announcements or malicious use of the vulnerability that is described in this advisory.


## 
Source 
  * This vulnerability was found during internal security testing.
